    response: Okay, so I need to figure out the significance of the nodes of Ranvier based on the provided documents. Let me start by recalling what I know about Ranvier nodes. From my biology class, I remember that Ranvier nodes are part of the nervous system, specifically in the myelin sheath along axons. They are important for the conduction of nerve impulses because they prevent the depolarization from spreading along the axon membrane, which would otherwise cause the signal to die out quickly.

Looking at the documents, I see that Context 1 talks about the structure of Ranvier nodes. It mentions that each node is flanked by paranodal regions with glial loops attached. The internodes are the myelin segments, and the nodes themselves are regions where the myelinating cells are organized. The nodes are surrounded by microvilli from Schwann cells in the PNS and perinodal extensions from astrocytes in the CNS. This structure helps in maintaining the myelin sheath and the proper spacing of nodes, which is crucial for efficient signal transmission.
